## 1. Architecture — subscription + daemon schedule + push on change

★The client **subscribes to the set of companies it wants to see**★ — one declarative set per connection, the last message is the state. The daemon counts cooldown and reset **only for subscribed companies**, queries by itself when the deadline comes, and pushes the changed snapshot **only to connections that subscribed to that company** right away — a pickup application changed something (merged per company over 1 second) · a query started · a query ended · an expiry latch. When a company newly enters the set, that connection gets the current snapshot immediately. With no subscription there is no query (「안 보이면 조회 안 함」). ⟳ and the LLM bus remain requests — the bus `usage.get` decides at request time even when nobody subscribes. There is no 60-second frontend poll (user decision 2026-09-27 — 「서버쪽에 관리가 들어간이상 15분 쿨타임도 서버쪽에서 관리하는게 맞아보임」 · replaces §3 #34). The value-change push itself is amendment 1 (user decision 2026-09-27 — 「이왕 빨리 전달할 수 있으면 즉각즉각」). Multi-client is out of scope (§6 #15).

```
agent ── decoder ─(UsageGate: 기본 계정 에이전트만)─ take_usage() ─► pump ─► StatusSink::usage_observed (신설 · 기본 no-op)
         backend::usage_probes() = [claude, codex] — 각 probe 가 key()·policy()·query() 를 가진다(벤더 지식은 backend 안)
           ├ claude : 짧은 claude -p 에 get_usage 한 줄 (A — 확정)
           └ codex  : 짧은 codex app-server --stdio 에 initialize → account/rateLimits/read
         실패 = ProbeFailure{error: ProbeError, detail: Option<UsageDetail>} — 상류 원문은 로그가 못 찍는 전용 칸(4-0b)
daemon ─ MessagingFlushSink → UsageObserveSink(신설 decorator — 쌓지 않는다) → DaemonStatusSink
                                   └ pump 스레드에서 곧장 UsageService::observe(obs) ─► 책에 적용 · 바뀌었으면 발행(회사당 1초 합침)
         UsageService
           구독 명부 = ConnId → (회사 집합, UsageOutlet) — on_connect 빈 칸 · UsageSubscribe 가 집합 전량 교체 · on_disconnect 지움
           스케줄러 = 분리 std 스레드 usage-scheduler — 구독된 회사의 next_auto · resets_at · 합침 기한 · 깨우기 중 가장 이른 때 깬다
           request(vendor, RequestKind) → Option<UsageAnswer{snapshot, served}> : ⟳(async · 연결 태스크) — 캐시 판정 → 즉시 답 | 조회 시작·합류 → REPLY_WAIT_MAX 안에서 기다림 · 모르는 회사 = None
           request_blocking(…) : 버스 — 같은 판정 · 기다림 = std sync_channel(1) + recv_timeout(REPLY_WAIT_MAX) · 시한이면 캐시 + in_flight=true · served Cached
           조회 = 분리된 std 스레드(완료 가드 — 적용·발행·깨우기) · UsageClock(mono·wall) · ~~RejectStore(usage_rejects.json — 조립 때 먼저 읽음)~~ (ADR-0284 로 걷음 — 2026-10-08 · 거절 기한은 메모리에만)
           발행 = 명부 락 안에서 대상(출구 + 그 연결의 지금 집합 사본) 수집 → 락 놓고 → 대상이 실은 서로 다른 집합마다 JSON 한 번(UsageEncoder(snapshot, subscribed) → Option — 실물 = AgentEvent::UsageLimitsUpdated{snapshot, subscribed} · None = 그 몫 건너뜀) → 그 회사를 구독한 연결의 UsageOutlet 마다 → 그 연결의 FrameSink::try_send
         입구 → 같은 서비스: wire UsageSubscribe{vendors}(셸, 줄 안) · RefreshUsageLimits{vendor}(⟳, Detached) · 버스 usage.get·usage.refresh{backend}(LLM, blocking)
shell ── usage_interest: 관심 = interest_from_layout(레이아웃, 숨긴 창) — 보이는 창(트레이 숨김 아님 · 최소화 = 보임)의 활성 탭에 있는 사용량 슬롯이 켠 회사의 합(개정 6)
         레이아웃·보임이 바뀌는 자리(RouterSubs::resync · 팝업 소멸 · 트레이 숨김/보임)마다 ViewManager 락 안에서 재계산 — 웹뷰는 관심을 보고하지 않는다
         UsageSubscribe 는 연결 태스크 한 줄에서만 — 보낼 때 관심을 읽음 · sent 와 비교(늘면 즉시 · 줄면 1.5초 뒤) · 새 소켓마다 · cmd_tx 저장 직후 한 번 더 · ⟳ 앞에 강제 한 장
         대조 = 스냅숏의 subscribed ≠ 바라는 집합(줄임 대기 중 = sent · 아니면 관심) → 관심 한 장 + sent := 관심
         socket_epoch 별 회사당 최고 revision 스냅숏 + 받은 시각 캐시 → get_usage_snapshot 으로 웹뷰가 당김(상대 칸 보정)
         방송 = emit_broadcast 호출 자리(connection.rs:1015)의 사용량 팔 → "usage-limits-updated"{labels = 사용량 슬롯이 있는 창, socket_epoch, snapshot}
         SlotContent::Usage{show_claude, show_codex} · layout.setSlotContent(+show_*) — 켠 회사는 셸의 슬롯 내용에 산다
front ── 관심을 계산·보고하지 않는다 · 방송 잇기 먼저 → agentClient.getUsageSnapshot()(셸 캐시 {socketEpoch, snapshots}) — 잇기 직후 한 번 + 사용량 슬롯 마운트 때
         ⟳ = refresh(답 = 기존 Ack — pending 만 풂 · 값은 방송으로) · 방송·pull → usageStore(socketEpoch 가 커질 때만 revision 잊음 · revision 이 더 작으면 버림)
```

### 1-1. agent — neutral types (new `crates/engram-dashboard-agent/src/usage/` — no vendor names or policy)

| Type | Shape · meaning |
|---|---|
| `UsageKey` | `vendor: UsageVendorKey` (opaque word — ★the canonical one is the single `&'static str` that `usage_probe_for(word)?.key()` gives★, regardless of the case of the incoming word) + `account: UsageAccountKey` (`String`, always `"default"` for now — D13, §3 #33). The daemon does not interpret this key |
| `UsagePolicy` | `cooldown: Duration` · `timeout: Duration` — values are set by each vendor file (§1-3) |
| `WindowObs` · `ScopedWindowObs` | `used_pct: Option<f64>` (0–100 normalized, §3 #32) · `resets_at: Option<i64>` (epoch seconds) · missing ≠ 0 (R21) · per-model weekly window = `label` + `WindowObs` (D8) |
| `UsageObservation` | `vendor: UsageVendorKey` · `five_hour`/`weekly: Option<WindowObs>` · `model_scoped: Option<Vec<…>>` (None = not carried, Some = full replacement) · `plan: Option<String>` · `source: Passive\|Active` (★amendment 12: kept — producers and tests carry and read it, but the merge and state rules do not look at this cell · removal is a separate later item, §3 #88★) · `limits_unavailable` (4-0 = `bool` `usage/mod.rs:119-125` → ★4-0b = `Option<UsageDetail>`★ — `Some` = the query succeeded but upstream answered 「이 계정엔 한도 정보가 없다」 + its basis · when `Some`, every window cell is None · pickup (decoder) observations are always `None`). The observation time is not carried — the daemon stamps it (the decoder has no clock — `backend/codex/decoder.rs:13-14`) |
| `ProbeError` | `NotInstalled` · `Unauthenticated` · `RateLimited{retry_after: Option<Duration>}` · `Unsupported` · `Timeout` · `Spawn(String)` · `Io(String)` · `Parse(String)` · `Upstream(String)` — folding into the visible state is §1-4. String cells, as now, hold **only sentences we wrote** (they go to the log) |
| `ProbeFailure` (4-0b) | `{error: ProbeError, detail: Option<UsageDetail>}` · `From<ProbeError>` (`detail: None`) — the spawner seam (`ProbeSpawner`·`ProbeChild`) stays `ProbeError` as is, so the `?` inside the querier does not change. `Display` = the `Display` of `error` + only `detail.kind` (no raw text) |
| `UsageDetail` (4-0b) | `{kind: &'static str, code: Option<i64>, upstream: Option<UpstreamText>}` — `kind` = classification word (the producer decides: neutral failures `timeout`·`spawn`·`io`·`parse`·`not_installed`·`unauthenticated`·`rate_limited`·`unsupported`·`upstream` = the `ProbeError::kind_word()` of `usage/` · vendor response classifications `rate_limits_null`·`claude_error`·`rpc_error`·`limits_unavailable` = each vendor querier). When `detail` is absent, the receiver fills in the single `error.kind_word()` |
| `UpstreamText` (4-0b) | Upstream raw-text newtype — the constructor `new` applies base `mask_secrets` (`base/src/logging/mod.rs:49`) → replaces control and bidirectional formatting characters — whitespace-like control characters (`\t`·`\n`·`\r`·`\x0B`·`\x0C`·U+0085·U+2028·U+2029) become a blank, everything else becomes U+FFFD (order: mask → replace → 200 characters · the decision uses the same character set as the display cell `display_text`) → truncates at 200 characters on a character boundary. Where an upstream cell value is carried as is, use `non_blank` — `None` if empty or whitespace only. ★`Debug` prints only `UpstreamText(<N자>)` and has no `Display`★ — even if a log prints it with `{:?}`·`{}`, the raw text does not go out. The only way to take the raw text out = `as_str()` (only the wire conversion calls it) |
| `trait UsageProbe` | `key() -> UsageVendorKey` · `policy() -> UsagePolicy` · `query(&self, env: &ProbeEnv) -> Result<UsageObservation, ProbeFailure>` (blocking — 4-0b changes the error type from `ProbeError`) · `ProbeEnv` = `spawner: &dyn ProbeSpawner` + `deadline` (the deadline of one whole query) + `scratch_root` (the parent folder the daemon gives — §1-3 temp folder) |
| `trait ProbeSpawner` / `ProbeChild` | `spawn(&ProbeCommand)` → `write_line(deadline)` · `read_line(deadline)` · `wait_exit(deadline)` — ★all three actions fall under the same deadline★: write and read use a helper thread + `recv_timeout`, exit wait polls `try_wait` until the deadline (a child that closed stdout and is still alive is also cut off at the deadline). When the deadline passes, the whole tree is killed. Drop = tree kill + a short bounded exit wait (`KILL_WAIT = 2초`). `ProbeCommand{program, args, cwd, env_set, env_remove}` — the agent's `CommandSpec` (`types.rs:185-191`) only adds env (`transport/stdio.rs:82-84`), so it is kept separate |
| `OsProbeSpawner` (`usage/process.rs`) | The shape of `transport/stdio.rs:79-117` (three pipes) + a stdout line reader thread + stderr drain (the content is discarded and only a few tail lines covered with base `mask_secrets` are kept — ★the log gets only the line count★ `usage/process.rs:13`·`:1002-1003`). ★The OS branches are inside this one module★ (「플랫폼 중립」): launch flag (Windows `CREATE_NO_WINDOW`) · tree kill (Windows = `JobObjectHandle` `platform/windows.rs:19-77`, KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE `:35` · POSIX = new process group + `killpg`) · exit code interpretation (§1-3 not installed) |
| `UsageGate` | Decoder wrapper — when `collect=false`, it always empties `take_usage()` (§1-2 non-default profile) |
| `StatusSink::usage_observed(&self, _obs)` | Next to `turn_ended` of `types.rs:878-900`, default no-op. The contract is the same — non-blocking and non-reentrant, called on the pump thread (non-blocking = no I/O and no await — the daemon side takes short locks one after another and sends **after releasing**: book → release → registry (collect targets) → release → (if there are targets) JSON once → `try_send` per connection · scheduler wake `try_send` — the same shape as the existing fan-out `net/src/ws.rs:207-221` (「맵을 잠근 채 보내지 않는다」) · §1-4 lock order per path) |

### 1-2. agent — pickup (passive)

- **Exit = StatusSink hook, transport = pump drain.** The decoder collects observations in its own `Vec` and hands them out through the new trait method `OutputDecoder::take_usage(&mut self) -> Vec<UsageObservation>` (default empty vector — `transport/mod.rs:34-41`). The pump empties it after every `decode`·`flush` and passes it to `status_sink.usage_observed(obs)` (which `output_core.rs:65` already holds). Pump places touched = `transport/stdio.rs:289-294`·`:312-316` · `backend/codex/transport.rs:2231-2234`·`:2323`.
- **Why the hook alone does not fit:** the decoder is created inside `open_spawn` (`backend/claude/mod.rs:411` · `backend/codex/mod.rs:1067`), moved into the pump, and only `OutputEvent` is handed back. To hand it a port, a cell would be added to `open_spawn`, three implementations and about ten call sites (`backend/mod.rs:1555` · `backend/codex/mod.rs:2381·2456·2575·2748·3529·3626` · `manager.rs:1479·3283·3307`) would change with it. Drain keeps the decoder pure.
- **A non-default profile does not pick up:** `open_spawn` looks at `spec.env`, and if the account env (Claude `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` · Codex `CODEX_HOME`) differs from the daemon's own value, the decoder is wrapped in `UsageGate{collect: false}`. The decision function lives in each vendor's `usage.rs` (vendor knowledge). The `output_decoder` (`backend/mod.rs:587`) signature does not change.
- **No-panic parsing (shared by pickup and query):** the release is `panic = "abort"` (root `Cargo.toml:35`) and the shape is EXPERIMENTAL — no `unwrap`·`expect`·indexing on external data, number conversion with `checked`/`try_from`. Non-finite (NaN·inf) utilization → that cell None · negative or over 100 → clamped · out-of-range epoch · not ISO · wrong type → that cell None (the whole window is not made a failure) · if the envelope itself is broken, a query is `Parse`, and pickup drops that line. ★The daemon's time arithmetic is on the same list too (amendment 3)★ — scheduler sleeps and deadlines use only saturating/`checked` arithmetic · the reset term is only a reset that has not yet come, as integer seconds clamped to (0, `SCHEDULE_MAX_SLEEP`] (`resets_at` is at most year 9999 — `usage/normalize.rs:18`) · if `wall()` is before 1970 it is negative (floor of seconds — no panic) · wake-up uses a non-blocking `try_send` (§1-4 scheduler).
- **Claude** — new `backend/claude/usage.rs`. `mod.rs` changes only in passing `"rate_limit_event"` detached from the skip arm (`:834`) (one observation-vector cell added to the `consume_line` `:744` signature).
  - `rate_limit_info.rateLimitType ∈ {five_hour, seven_day}` + `utilization` (0–1 → ×100, clamped to [0,100]) + `resetsAt` (epoch seconds) → that window. Unknown types are dropped (debug once). `unifiedWindows.{five_hour,seven_day}.{utilization,resetsAt}` is also accepted loosely (R20) — if both have it, `unifiedWindows` wins, and if a cell type is wrong only that cell is dropped.
  - Old shape (`fixtures/claude_text.jsonl:3` — no `utilization`) → `used_pct: None, resets_at: Some` (§1-5). The `mod.rs:2757` test (`rate_limit_info:{}` → skip) stays green as is.
- **Codex** — new `backend/codex/usage.rs`. Adds the `account/rateLimits/updated` arm to `decoder.rs:443-481`, removes it from `KNOWN_UNTRANSLATED_METHODS` (`:135`), and fixes the count sentence (`:125-126`). In `protocol.rs`, the method constant and the inbound struct (`RateLimitSnapshot`·`RateLimitWindow{usedPercent, windowDurationMins, resetsAt}` — only the cells we read, no `deny_unknown_fields` — `protocol.rs:11-20`). Window classification = `windowDurationMins` 300 → 5-hour · 10080 → weekly · anything else dropped (R18). Null window = `None`. `OutputEvent` is not emitted (`decoder.rs:18-25`).

### 1-3. agent — active query (probe) · vendor policy is inside the backend

- **One registry:** put `backend::usage_probes() -> [&'static dyn UsageProbe; 2]` + `backend::usage_probe_for(word: &str)` (case-insensitive — `commands.rs:376` precedent) next to the static backend table (`backend/mod.rs:660-671`) — ★vendor names are written only in that table, `backend_for` and the singleton declarations★ (`backend/mod.rs:677-678` · ADR-0004). The probe singleton lives in each vendor's `usage.rs`. No cell is added to the `AgentBackend` trait (no double dispatch).
- **Claude A — confirmed (D2)** (`backend/claude/usage.rs` — `CLAUDE_COOLDOWN = 15분` (R15) · `CLAUDE_PROBE_TIMEOUT = 15초` (R30))
  - Command = `console_command(CLAUDE_PROGRAM, args)` (`mod.rs:48` · `backend/mod.rs:44-57` — production is `cmd.exe /c claude`). args = `-p --input-format stream-json --output-format stream-json --replay-user-messages --verbose --no-session-persistence --settings <파일> --strict-mcp-config --mcp-config <파일>`. `--permission-mode bypassPermissions` is left out (there is no prompt to ask). The two settings are not inline JSON but file paths written into a temp folder freshly made for each query — this avoids JSON quoting through `cmd.exe`. `--mcp-config <경로>` is the form the production spawn already uses (`mod.rs:226-230`) · whether `--settings` accepts a path is checked in the CLI help (unverified — if it does not, inline + smoke). cwd = that temp folder.
  - **Temp folder (shared by both vendors — `ScratchDir` of `usage/process.rs`):** under `scratch_root` (= `usage-probe/` of the daemon data folder), with a random name (uuid v4), by **exclusive `create_dir`** (fails if it already exists → a new name) — because it holds the `--mcp-config` file that claude trusts and reads, so a folder pre-planted by someone else is not reused. When done: tree kill → wait for the child to exit within a bound (`KILL_WAIT` — Windows cannot delete an open file) then delete, and a delete failure is ignored. Leftover folders are swept at daemon start — next to `control::mcp_config::sweep_stale_configs(&data_dir)` (`lib.rs:535`), safe because there is one daemon per data folder.
  - env = from the daemon env, strip `CLAUDE*` (case-insensitive) but keep the allowlist `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`·`CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` + the same four keys as the probe `MAX_THINKING_TOKENS=8000 ENABLE_CLAUDEAI_MCP_SERVERS=false CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_CONNECT_IDE=0 CLAUDE_CODE_IDE_SKIP_AUTO_INSTALL=1` (probe = `get-usage-probe.mjs:31-40` — that one strips all of `CLAUDE*`).
  - ★A real Windows smoke with the production command as is is the Stage 3 completion condition★ — the probe launched `claude.exe` directly (`get-usage-probe.mjs:16`), and arguments, env and shim differ. It is `#[ignore]` so CI does not run it, but run it locally and write the result in the step-log.
  - Write one line of `{"type":"control_request","request_id":<uuid v4>,"request":{"subtype":"get_usage","skip_behaviors":true}}` and, keeping stdin open, read up to the line where `type=="control_response"` and the `request_id` matches (other lines are dropped). Kill on receipt.
  - Interpretation (4-0 amendment — `backend/claude/usage_probe.rs:290-355`): `response.subtype=="success"` → `response.response` · `.rate_limits.five_hour`/`.seven_day.{utilization 0–100, resets_at ISO 8601}` · `model_scoped[]` and `seven_day_opus`/`_sonnet`/`_oauth_apps` that hold a value → per-model windows · `subscription_type` → plan. ★The meaning of the two cells is decided by the CLI 2.1.280 response schema description★ — `rate_limits_available` = 「plan 한도가 적용되지 않으면 `false`(API 키·Bedrock·Vertex·profile 권한 없는 토큰) — 그때 `rate_limits` 는 null」 · `rate_limits` = 「CLI 가 받아 오지 못하면 null(이 경로에 plan 없음 · profile 권한 없는 토큰)」.
    - When `rate_limits_available` is bool `false` → observation `limits_unavailable = Some(UsageDetail{kind: "limits_unavailable", upstream: 원인 칸})` (all windows None · `rate_limits` is not read whatever its shape · plan is read) → 「한도 정보 없음」 (§1-4 `Unavailable`). The cause cell = `rate_limits_available: false, subscription_type: <받은 값 그대로 — null 포함>`. A logged-out CLI also answers in this shape (measured 2026-09-27 — `agent/tests/usage_probe_smoke.rs:72` `claude_logged_out_answer_shape`).
    - Otherwise (`true` · cell absent · not bool = absent) when `rate_limits` is an explicit null → `ProbeError::Upstream` + `UsageDetail{kind: "rate_limits_null", upstream: "rate_limits_available: <받은 값 그대로 — 칸이 없으면 그 칸은 뺀다>, rate_limits: null"}` (= query failure · value kept). ★Even when the CLI-side query receives a 429, it arrives as this null rather than an error subtype (CLI code read — not measured)★ → on 2.1.280 `Rejected` is unreachable, it shows as `Failed` and is retried at the normal cooldown (§6 #21). ★The CLI knows 「한도에 걸렸나」 (`rateLimitedVia`) internally and writes it on its own `/usage` screen, but does not carry it in the `get_usage` response★ — the response object is only `{session, subscription_type, rate_limits_available, rate_limits, behaviors}` (CLI 2.1.280 code read 2026-09-27 — not measured). So a common failure has no upstream error type, and the raw text = the two received cells as they are.
    - If the `rate_limits` cell is missing or is neither an object nor null → `Parse` (so that a response with a changed shape does not enter as a success and erase values) — detail is only `kind`.
  - Error classification (one function `classify_error` `usage_probe.rs:417-464`, §3 #6): limit wording → `RateLimited{None}` · login/auth wording → `Unauthenticated` · "not supported in this context" → `Unsupported` · anything else → `Upstream`. ★All four are `UsageDetail{kind: "claude_error", upstream: 응답 `error` 문자열 그대로}` (4-0b)★. The wording table is an estimate (§6 #4) — 2.1.280 does not return a limit as an error subtype, so the limit wording table is kept **for when another version answers with an error subtype**. It is not sent to a running agent process (D7).
  - ★What goes into the raw-text cell = only the values of the named cells★ (Claude `error` · the two cells above · Codex `error.message`) — the response line and stderr are not carried whole (the Codex response carries `accountId` — below). The raw text's destinations = only the UI and LLM read paths, logging forbidden (§1-4 「실패 추적」).
- **Not installed:** if stdout EOF is seen with no response line, the exit status is received with `wait_exit(deadline)` and interpreted — Windows = `cmd.exe` exit code 9009 · POSIX = spawn `NotFound` → `NotInstalled` (inside `usage/process.rs`). If it does not end by the deadline, `Timeout` + tree kill.
- **Swap alternative B** (only when A breaks — D2): a second implementation behind the same `UsageProbe` seam. What changes = ① the first TLS dependency + the daemon's first internet send (HTTPS through an injected port) ② one OS-branched function to find credentials (Windows·Linux = the file in `config_dir()` `mod.rs:993` · macOS = Keychain · env token) ③ token handling (read only · no storage · no log — D5). The rest of the design is rewritten in the TRD at the time of the switch.
- **Codex** (`backend/codex/usage.rs` — `CODEX_COOLDOWN = 15분` (R15 — user decision 2026-09-27, unified with Claude) · `CODEX_PROBE_TIMEOUT = 30초` = hang prevention (normally under 1 second measured · about 10 seconds observed once in resuming a conversation — safety margin))
  - Command = `console_command(CODEX_PROGRAM, [APP_SERVER_SUBCOMMAND, APP_SERVER_STDIO_FLAG])` (`mod.rs:169`·`:461-462`·`:971`). env = inherit the daemon env. cwd = a temp folder made the same way.
  - **Reuse:** `classify` (`:151`)·`request_line`·`notification_line`·`error_response_line` (`:278-310`)·`InitializeParams`/`ClientInfo`/`InitializeResponse` (`:316-341`)·`method::INITIALIZE`/`INITIALIZED` + `CLIENT_NAME` (`transport.rs:288` — moved to `protocol.rs`) of `protocol.rs`. **Not reused:** `request_blocking`·`Pending`·`Waiter` (`transport.rs:742-797`·`:426`) — they are tied to the writer thread and shared state of the conversation channel.
  - Order = `initialize` (id 1) → wait for the response (a server request is refused with `error_response_line` — the same meaning as `transport.rs:1903` `refuse` · a notification is dropped) → `initialized` → `account/rateLimits/read` (id 2) → response → kill.
  - Interpretation = classify the top-level snapshot `primary`/`secondary` by window length + `planType` → plan + `rateLimitsByLimitId` non-default bucket weekly window → per-model window (label = `limitName`, the limit id if absent). RPC error → `Unauthenticated` if it is auth wording, otherwise `Upstream` — both `UsageDetail{kind: "rpc_error", code: Some(error.code), upstream: error.message 그대로}` (4-0b).
  - ★The response carries `accountId` — never leave it in a log★ (including the stderr and parse-error tails). It is the future source of `account_key` on the Codex side, but for now `"default"` as is.

### 1-4. daemon — `UsageService` (new `crates/engram-dashboard-daemon/src/usage_service/` — no vendor names or policy)

`book.rs` (a pure state machine — one cell per `UsageKey` · a monotonic `revision: u64` per cell · a merge state per cell) · `watch.rs` (the subscription registry — connection → company set + exit port `UsageOutlet`) · `schedule.rs` (the scheduler — one run = copy of the registry union → book `plan_tick` (the pure decision lives in `book.rs` — measured with a fake clock) → `carry_out` + a thin thread driver · entry point `spawn_scheduler` — 4d2b) · `mod.rs` (request · join · query driving · pickup application · emit · query-thread start seam `ProbeThreads` — the real `OsProbeThreads` = a detached std thread `usage-probe` · it exists to test the thread-start `Err` · construction = `UsageService::new(UsageParts{probes, spawner, scratch_root, threads, rejects, clock, encoder}) -> (Arc<Self>, Receiver<()>)` — built by holding its own `Weak` with `Arc::new_cyclic`, and the receiving end is the scheduler thread's share · 4d2a·#89) · `clock.rs` · ~~`reject_store.rs`~~ (removed by ADR-0284 — 2026-10-08) · `observe.rs` (decorator `UsageObserveSink` — no stacking, straight to the service). The key list, cooldown and deadline are received from `backend::usage_probes()` — there is no vendor name in the daemon code. ★The only timer is the scheduler (amendment 2)★ — the old 「태스크·타이머 없음 · 판정은 요청 때」 is replaced (§3 #34·#58).

**Deadline formula — only one (R29):** `next_auto = max(max(last_query, fresh_reset) + policy.cooldown, reject_until)` (amendment 12 — old name `passive_reset`) · when there is no reference point (the first subscription or request after startup) `reject_until` (immediately if absent). The refusal end and reset elapsed are not in the formula, so they cannot bring a query forward — the scheduler waking at the reset time is to set the latch, not to query. Daemon constants = `REPLY_WAIT_MAX = 5초` (§3 #16 · lives in `mod.rs` — it is not `book.rs`, 4d2a·#89) · `REJECT_FALLBACK = 5분` (D4) · `REJECT_MAX = 24시간` (§3 #37) · ~~`REFRESH_MIN_SPACING = 30초` (§3 #38)~~ (deleted in amendment 15 — §3 #102) · `RESET_SAME_TOLERANCE = 60초` (§3 #46) · `USAGE_PUBLISH_COALESCE = 1초` (§3 #60) · `SCHEDULE_MAX_SLEEP = 60초` (§3 #58).

**revision (per cell — §3 #49):** +1 whenever anything in the cell changes — a pickup application changed something · a query started (`in_flight`) · a query completed · a latch set (anywhere: request, pickup, scheduler, subscription add) (amendment 9 — #85). ★A change = the values the snapshot carries (window `used_pct`·`resets_at`·`expired`·~~`source`~~ (amendment 12 — removed §3 #88) · per-model · plan · state · state detail) or `next_auto` differs★ — picking up the same value again so that only its age is fresher is not a change (no emit · the new age is carried by the next snapshot). ★When the revision rises, emit — no exceptions★ (amendment 2 — replaces the old 「조회 시작·요청 때 래치는 +1 만 하고 발행 안 함」: with the poll gone, broadcast is the only way `in_flight` and expiry can reach anyone). Only pickup is merged to once per second per company (「발행」 below). Both the answer and the broadcast carry it (§1-6).

| Request (per company — ⟳ and the bus) | Decision | Answer (bus row — the wire ⟳ sends only the existing `Ack` once, at the point where this decision and waiting end · the value goes to subscribers by broadcast, amendment 6) |
|---|---|---|
| `get` (bus `usage.get` only — there is no wire `get` §3 #67), `now < next_auto` | cache | immediate cache (`served = Cached`) |
| `refresh`, `now < reject_until` | refusal | cache + `Rejected{retry_in_secs}` — no query goes out (R24) · a visible refusal = 「거절됨 — N분 뒤」 + ⟳ disabled · a refusal while a value has arrived and shows as `Ready` = ⟳ quietly answers from cache (no query goes out · no indication — §3 #88 ⓑ C) |
| ~~`refresh`, within 30 seconds after the previous query ended~~ | ~~interval (간격)~~ | ~~cache (`Cached`) — no query goes out (D11 amendment)~~ ★amendment 15 — this row is removed (user decision 2026-09-29 · §3 #102): if one is in progress, the join in the row below blocks it, and if in refusal, the row above blocks it★ |
| `get` deadline passed · other `refresh` | start a query (`refresh` ignores the cooldown — D11), join if one is already in progress | on completion the new state (`Fresh`) · on failure cache + failure state · past `REPLY_WAIT_MAX` cache + `in_flight=true` (the query continues — when it ends it reaches subscribers by broadcast) |

| Event | Action |
|---|---|
| Subscription set replacement (구독 집합 교체 · `UsageSubscribe` — one connection) | ① **Before** the registry lock, evaluate the latch for each company in the requested set (`wall` — book lock only) — if set, revision +1 → ordinary emit (to that company's existing subscribers · amendment 3) ② registry lock → replace the whole set **only if** that connection's cell **exists** (a cell is created by `on_connect` and removed by `on_disconnect` — if absent, discard) → take a snapshot for each newly added company (the book lock only for that moment) + a copy of that connection's set **after** the replacement (`subscribed` — amendment 6) → **release the registry lock** → JSON once per snapshot (amendment 4 — encoding is outside the lock) → one by one to that connection's exit → scheduler wake (if that company's `next_auto ≤ now`, the scheduler starts a query). The first snapshot is not sent for a company that dropped out or stayed the same (the latch emit in ① is an ordinary emit, so separate). An empty set = unsubscribe |
| Scheduler wake (§3 #58) | for each subscribed company, the pure `plan_tick` (`book.rs`) that can be measured with a fake clock: not `in_flight` ∧ `now ≥ next_auto` → start a query (the **same** start, join and completion guard as the request path) · a window that is `wall ≥ resets_at` → latch · a waiting emit whose merge deadline has arrived → emit. The next wake time it returns = the `next_auto` of a subscribed company (excluded if in progress) · the `resets_at` of a latch not yet set (wall-clock difference → `mono`) · the `reject_until` of the `Rejected` cell — the refusal end is carved with `eval_time` (amendment 9 — #85) · the earliest of the merge deadlines, capped at `SCHEDULE_MAX_SLEEP`. With no subscribed company there is no query and no deadline |
| Request arrives (before the decision) | set the **expiry latch** (once — R32) on a window that is `wall_now ≥ resets_at` — if set, revision +1 → emit (if there are subscribers). The query time is not changed |
| Query start (shared by request and scheduler) | `in_flight = true` → revision +1 → snapshot → release lock → emit — this is the only way 「갱신 중」 reaches anyone (no poll) |
| Pickup observation (줍기 관측) (partial) — ★straight from the pump thread (user decision 2026-09-27 — replaces the old 「대기 맵 + 요청 때 병합」)★ | book lock → stamp `mono` → merge per window (R16·~~D18~~ — `merge_window`, one caller · ★amendment 12: a successful active query also goes through this one merge — regardless of source, §3 #88★): **a window absent from the observation = kept** · `used_pct` Some → replace + release that window's latch + fresh · `resets_at` is replaced only when Some (None = not carried → kept) (amendment 9 — #85) · when `used_pct` is None, ★the % is erased only when the old and new `resets_at` are **both Some** and the difference > `RESET_SAME_TOLERANCE`★ — difference ≤ 60 seconds → kept · old None → new Some = learning the reset (% kept) · new None = not carried (kept) (§3 #46). When fresh stands for both the 5-hour and weekly windows after the reference point, `fresh_reset = 이 관측의 mono`, fresh initialization (D12) — `reject_until` is not touched. State: ~~if `used_pct` Some arrives while `Unavailable` → `Ready` (evidence that this account has a limit · detail erased) · pickup does not change the other states (`Failed`·`NeedsLogin`·`NotInstalled`·`Rejected`) (the value is still drawn — R22)~~ ★replaced (amendment 12 — §3 #88)★: an observation that carries even one window value (% or reset) → `Ready` from any state (detail erased) · `reject_until` is left as is (only the visible 「거절됨」 disappears — no automatic or ⟳ query before the deadline). Latch evaluation (래치 평가 · `wall`) → if anything changed, revision +1 → snapshot → **release the lock** → emit (merge rule — 「발행」 below). If nothing changed, no emit. If `next_auto` changed (D12), wake the scheduler |
| Active success (full) | ~~replace both windows, per-model windows and plan in full — **null or a missing window = "absent"** (under R16 · D18) · release all latches~~ ★replaced (amendment 12 — §3 #88)★: the value is **the same merge** as the 「줍기 관측」 row above — only the windows and cells carried are overwritten, and null or missing ones are kept · per-model windows and plan are replaced whole when carried, otherwise kept · a latch is released only for a window that carried a new % · the passed-window indication (#3·#46·#85 F·H) and D12 are the same · state `Ready` (detail erased) · refusal deadline (`reject_until`) erased (amendment 9 — #85). No ordering rule with pickup is set — the later write wins (user decision 2026-09-27, §6 #17). Sequential application stands by structure (pickup and completion are applied one after another inside the same book lock) |
| Active success — no limit information (한도 정보 없음 · `limits_unavailable` Some, 4-0·4-0b) | clear all window values (both windows · per-model) · plan = ~~observed value~~ replaced if the observation carries it, kept as held if not (amendment 12 — unified rule · §3 #88) · release all latches · state `Unavailable` + detail = the observation's detail · refusal deadline (`reject_until`) erased (amendment 9 — #85). ★It is not a failure★ — `last_query` is stamped by the completion guard, and the next automatic query = the normal cooldown |
| Active failure | values kept (R22) · state = the table below + detail = the detail of `ProbeFailure` (only `kind_word()` if absent) · `RateLimited` → `reject_until = now + min(retry_after, REJECT_MAX)` (absent · broken · past · 0 → `REJECT_FALLBACK` — R23, addition is `checked_add`) + store |
| Query end (조회 끝) (success · failure · deadline) | ★the completion guard bears it★ — after the Drop guard of the query-running thread upgrades `Weak<UsageService>` (failure = the service is already gone → discard the result · amendment 3), apply the result **inside the book lock** (the three rows above) · `last_query = now` (D11) · `in_flight = false` · revision +1 → snapshot → **release the lock** → emit (on every completion — `in_flight` was released even if nothing changed) → wake the scheduler (the next `next_auto`) → wake joiners (async = `watch` generation +1 · bus = send to each registered std sender). The joiner's answer, once woken, appears after that, so its revision ≥ the emitted one. Even if a request gave up at `REPLY_WAIT_MAX`, no `in_flight` remains. It always runs within the deadline (write · read · exit all) + `KILL_WAIT` + temp-folder delete retries (about 0.2 seconds — 4d2a·#89) |
| Deadline exceeded | if the deadline is hit anywhere in write · read · exit wait, tree kill · `Timeout` = counted as a failure · next deadline = the next cooldown (R30) |

| Visible state (per company — R31) | Trigger | Wording |
|---|---|---|
| `NotInstalled` | `ProbeError::NotInstalled` | 설치 안 됨 |
| `NeedsLogin` | `ProbeError::Unauthenticated` | 로그인 필요 |
| `Unavailable` (4-0 — the sixth wire state) | active success + `limits_unavailable` Some (Claude = only when `rate_limits_available` is bool `false` — logged out · API key · Bedrock · Vertex · token without profile permission) · no value · ~~when pickup `used_pct` arrives, `Ready`~~ when an observation carrying a window value (% or reset) arrives, `Ready` (regardless of source — amendment 12 §3 #88) | 「사용량 정보 없음」 family (user decision 2026-09-27 · exact wording is Stage 7 — §6 #20) |
| `Failed{next_attempt_in_secs: u64}` | `Timeout`·`Unsupported`·`Spawn`·`Io`·`Parse`·`Upstream` — Claude 2.1.280's `rate_limits: null` (no explicit `false` — including the CLI-side 429) also falls here as `Upstream` | query failed — next attempt HH:MM (= `next_auto`) |
| `Rejected{retry_in_secs}` | `ProbeError::RateLimited` (while `now < reject_until`) — after that `Failed{next_attempt_in_secs}` · ★amendment 12: if a window value comes in meanwhile and it becomes `Ready`, the refusal deadline lives only inside (it blocks automatic and ⟳ queries) and the refusal end does not turn `Ready` back to `Failed` (§3 #88)★ · ★unreachable on Claude 2.1.280★ (the 429 arrives as null, not as an error subtype — §1-3·§6 #21) | 거절됨 — N분 뒤 |

- **`next_attempt_in_secs` = `u64` (not `Option` — 4-0 decision · the code switch is Stage 4):** `next_auto` is always defined (if there is no reference point, `reject_until` or now) → `next_auto − now` saturates at 0.
- **Failure tracing (실패 추적) — classification word + upstream raw text (user decision 2026-09-27 — 「오류 문구 원문이 있으면 보내야지 … 이상한거 다 섞어서 보내지 말고 타입은 보내야지」 · 「유저가 최대한 추적할 수 있도록」):** all five states other than `Ready` carry a detail (the book cell holds the `UsageDetail` of the last failure or absence, and it stays even when `Rejected` ends and folds into `Failed` · erased when it becomes `Ready`). The wire carries it as `UsageStateDetail{kind, code, upstream}` (§1-6) — they are not mixed: our classification = `kind` · upstream number = `code` · upstream text = `upstream`. ★The raw text's destinations are only the UI (badge `aria-label`·`title` · popup top) and the LLM read path (the `usage.get` row) — it is not put in log files (logs may be shared · the after-deadline result log too — see 「버스」 below)★. Covering (base `mask_secrets`) + replacing control and format characters + the 200-character cap are done in one pass by the `UpstreamText` constructor (§1-1). ★This **deliberately changes** the rule at the head of `ProbeError` (「문자열 칸에 비밀을 싣지 않는다 … stderr 원문도 넣지 않는다」 `usage/probe.rs:24-27`)★ — the raw text does not live in that string cell but in a dedicated cell that `Display`/`Debug` for logging cannot print. 4-0b fixes that doc (「문자열 칸 = 로그로 간다 → 우리 문장만 · 상류 원문 = `UsageDetail.upstream` 전용 — 가림·치환·상한·로그 불가」).
- **Concurrency and locks:** `Mutex<UsageBook>` (4d2a·#89: what the lock wraps = `Desk{book, runs}` — `runs` = the number of finished queries per key `finished` · the last success number `last_ok` · blocking joiners · async joiners wait on one tokio `watch<u64>` (the finished count) per key) is a leaf lock — while holding it, no I/O, no await, no other lock, and no **emit** (the ADR-0006 principle — take the snapshot, release the lock, then emit). Holders = request decision · the later part of a request `close()` (book {latch · snapshot · `last_ok` check} → if a latch was set, emit outside the lock — 4d2a·#89) · pickup application (pump thread) · the completion guard (if the thread-start `Err`, straight from the requester or scheduler thread — book → release → emit·`watch`, #89 F) · scheduler `tick` · latch evaluation and snapshot on subscription replacement · ~~the snapshot for refusal storage~~ (removed by ADR-0284 — 2026-10-08) — all short in-memory sections, so the pump never waits on I/O. ★It tolerates poison (`PoisonError::into_inner`)★ — the pump thread must not panic (release `panic = "abort"` — root `Cargo.toml:35`). ★The registry lock (`watch.rs`) sits above the book — order `명부 → 책`★: a subscription replacement may briefly take the book while holding the registry (the book is still a leaf — there is no place that takes the registry while holding the book). ★Nothing is sent while holding the registry lock either (amendment 3 — ADR-0006 decision third line 「subscribers를 clone한 뒤 lock 미보유 상태로 send」)★ — inside the registry lock it only collects the target exits (`Arc`) and a copy of that connection's company set (`subscribed` — amendment 6), and after releasing the lock, if there are collected targets, it builds JSON once per distinct set the targets carry and does a non-blocking `try_send` per exit. ★Amendment 4 — encoding is also outside the lock (main decision)★: amendment 3 had 「명부 락 안에서 대상 수집 + JSON 한 번 직렬화」, but serialization was moved outside the lock — targets are still collected inside the lock, and any mismatch between the build and send of two emits and the application order that outside-the-lock allows is already told apart by the shell's highest-revision cache (§1-7) and the frontend revision (§1-8) (「발행」 below). The existing fan-out `net/src/ws.rs:207-221` has exactly this shape (「맵을 잠근 채 보내지 않는다」 — copy, then release the lock and send). So even if an exit (including a fake) calls the service again inside it — even subscription replacement or release — no lock is held and there is no deadlock. After a release, the exit copy held by an in-progress emit is dropped after that `try_send` once — it is the same transient copy as the subscribers copy of agent output emit (amendment 11 — #87). The registry lock also tolerates poison. The order per path = the table below. Even if two pump threads emit in an order different from the application order, the receiving side tells them apart by revision (§1-8). ~~Join = a `watch` generation number per key — the request that started and the request that joined wait for the same completion with `timeout(REPLY_WAIT_MAX)`.~~ ★4d2a·#89★: join = the request that started and the request that joined wait for the same completion until `REPLY_WAIT_MAX` — an async request (⟳ — connection task) waits on the per-key tokio `watch<u64>` (the finished query count), and a bus request (blocking) waits on the std `sync_channel(1)` it put on the joiner list with `recv_timeout` (`watch` is for async only). Companies do not wait for each other.

**Lock order per path (경로별 락 차례) (amendment 3)** — `X{…}` = while holding X · `→` = after releasing the previous lock. ~~There are only two places that hold locks nested (`명부{책{…}}` · `save_lock{책{…}}`) — in both the book is inner, and no path holds the registry and `save_lock` together, so there is no cycle.~~ ★There is only one place that holds locks nested (`명부{책{…}}` — the book is inner)★ — `save_lock` was removed together with refusal storage (removed by ADR-0284 — 2026-10-08 · §3 #109). 「보내기」 (send — exit `try_send`) · JSON encoding (amendment 4) · wake (`try_send`) · joiner wake · query-thread start~~ · file write~~ are in neither the book lock nor the registry lock. In the table below, 「대상」 (targets) = the exit copy + a copy of that connection's current company set (`subscribed` — amendment 6) · 「인코딩」 (encode) = only when there are collected targets, once per distinct `subscribed` the targets carry (once if there is one shell · if `None`, that share is skipped).

| Path (thread) | Lock order | After releasing the locks |
|---|---|---|
| Pickup application (pump) | book {`mono` · window merge · D12 · latch · revision · snapshot · merge decision} → registry {collect targets} | encode → `try_send` per exit · wake (`next_auto` changed · a merge wait newly stands) |
| Request decision (⟳·bus — connection task · blocking pool) | book {latch · decision · `in_flight` if starting · revision · snapshot · register joiner} → registry {targets} | encode → emit `try_send` · query-thread start · wake · waiting (`watch` / `recv_timeout`) |
| Later part of a request `close()` (⟳·bus — after waiting · 4d2a·#89) | book {latch · snapshot · `last_ok` check} → (if a latch was set) registry {targets} | (if a latch was set) encode → emit `try_send` |
| Completion guard (query — if thread-start `Err`, straight from the requester or scheduler thread, #89 F) | `Weak` upgrade → book {apply result · `last_query` · `in_flight = false` · `reject_until` · revision · snapshot · take out the joiner list} → registry {targets} | encode → `try_send` per exit → wake → joiner wake ~~→ (if refusal) the refusal storage below (amendment 9 — #85: if the refusal deadline was set, changed or erased — `reject_changed`) — last so that file I/O does not delay the answer~~ (removed by ADR-0284 — 2026-10-08) |
| Scheduler `tick` (usage-scheduler) | `Weak` upgrade → registry {union copy} → book {per company: query-start decision · latch · full merge waits · snapshots · next wake time} → registry {targets per snapshot} | encode per snapshot → `try_send` · query-thread start · release the upgraded `Arc` and `recv_timeout` |
| Subscription replacement (connection task — `InOrder`) | book {latch evaluation of the requested set · one emit per company that stood} → (if stood) registry {targets} → ★the latch emit goes out here — encode without a lock → `try_send`, **before** the registry replacement (the same as ① of the event table above 「구독 집합 교체」 · 4d1)★ → registry {replace the set only if the cell exists · newly added companies · book {snapshot per that company} · that connection's exit copy + the replaced set copy (amendment 6)} | encode per first snapshot → `try_send` (to that connection only) · wake |
| Release (`on_disconnect`) | registry {take out the cell} | withdraw the exit taken out (`revoke`) and drop it — **before** returning (port duty `net/src/frame_port.rs:212`) (amendment 11 — #87) |
| ~~Refusal storage (query — end of completion guard)~~ (removed by ADR-0284 — 2026-10-08) | ~~`save_lock` {book {all-cell refusal snapshot} → file write (tmp+rename)}~~ | — |

- **Subscription registry (`watch.rs` — amendment 2):** `ConnId → {vendors: BTreeSet<UsageVendorKey>, outlet: Arc<dyn UsageOutlet>}`. ★The only place that creates a cell is `on_connect`, the only place that removes one is `on_disconnect`, and `UsageSubscribe` edits **only cells that exist**★ — so a late `UsageSubscribe` that overlapped while `on_disconnect` was running (the abort overlap of the port contract — the very race the `agent_conn.rs:391-398` comment describes) cannot revive a removed cell (the attach·`refuse_if_detached` precedent of the command registry `command_roster.rs:8`·`:160`). Removal also drops the `FrameSink` copy held by the exit — port duty (`net/src/frame_port.rs:212` 「반환 시점까지 자기가 만든 사본을 전부 놓아야 한다」). Union = the sum of all sets in the registry — the scheduler reads it. Company words are normalized with `usage_probe_for(word)?.key()`, and a word with no querier is dropped (§1-6 key normalization). ★`subscribed` (amendment 6 — the shell's reconciliation §1-7)★: every usage event sent to a connection carries that connection cell's **current** `vendors`. There are two places that read it — inside the registry lock where an emit opens its targets (the set copy is taken together with the exit copy) and inside the registry lock where a subscription replacement takes the first snapshot (the **post**-replacement set). Both are memory copies only, and building and sending happen after the lock is released (the 「경로별 락 차례」 above as is — the book is still the inner leaf). Because sending is outside the lock, the `subscribed` received may be one step late — the shell reconciliation just sends the same set one more time, and a replacement with no newly added company issues no snapshot, so there is no feedback loop (§1-7 「대조」).
- **First snapshot order — relying on revision (amendment 2 · §3 #57):** do not imitate the replay→live of agent output (「subscribers lock 보유 중 replay 전송」 — a CLAUDE.md core invariant). ★The basis for no gap = 「등록 먼저, 스냅숏 나중」 + 「적용 먼저, 대상 열거 나중」★: a subscription replacement takes the book snapshot **after** editing the set inside the registry lock, and an emit opens the targets inside the registry lock **after** applying to the book (both send **after** releasing the registry lock — amendment 3. This proof relies only on who **saw** what (visibility order), so it stands as is, and all that sending outside the lock adds is the 「작은 revision 이 늦게」 below). When the two overlap, ① if the emit took the registry first, its application is already in the book and is in the snapshot the replacement takes, and ② if the replacement took it first, this connection is among the emit's targets — either way that connection receives that value or later. What remains is only that **the same value arrives twice** or **a smaller revision arrives late** (작은 revision 이 늦게), and the receiving side already tells them apart by revision (the same handling as the emit inversion of two pump threads). The replay style that sends while holding a lock would have to break the book and registry locks (no sending while holding — ADR-0006), so it is not used — unlike an output chunk, a snapshot is one sheet that is the whole state, so 「가장 높은 것이 이긴다」 is enough.
- **Scheduler (`schedule.rs` — amendment 2 · §3 #58):** one per daemon — a detached `std::thread` (name `usage-scheduler`) + a std `sync_channel(1)` wake channel. Entry point = `spawn_scheduler(&Arc<UsageService>, Receiver<()>) -> io::Result<JoinHandle<()>>` (4d2b — if the handle is dropped it keeps running detached · if `Err`, assembly leaves a note and continues: the service runs but everything time-driven stops — automatic queries · emits that go out when a deadline fills · recovery of unpaid emit debt (§3 #86). Requests (⟳·bus), pickup and subscription replacement keep running). Loop = hold the service with `Weak` and `upgrade` → `tick` (registry union copy → read `now` inside the book lock and `plan_tick` → `carry_out` outside the lock) → release the upgraded `Arc` → `recv_timeout(잠)` until the next wake time. A wake (one `()` cell) = subscription replacement · query end · pickup changed `next_auto` · a merge wait newly stands — ★`try_send` and `Full` is ignored (amendment 3)★: if one wake is already waiting it means the same thing, and the waker (including the pump thread) is not blocked. ★When the service is dropped the sender disappears too, so `Disconnected` → the thread ends by itself (since `Weak`, the thread does not hold the service)★ — the sender lives only inside the service, and the query thread also holds the service only as `Weak` (「조회 구동·종료」 below), so even with a blocked query in progress, dropping the service is `Disconnected` at once — for the same reason as the query thread, graceful shutdown (`lib.rs:754-782`) has no step, the runtime drop does not wait, and there is no risk of a tokio timer poll during shutdown (§3 #17·#39). ★The clock that `recv_timeout` measures is not `UsageClock::mono`★ (depending on the OS it may not count sleep time — §6 #2) — so on waking it always re-reads `mono()` to decide, and the 60-second cap bounds the lateness after resume and wall-clock shifts (reset-time conversion) to within 60 seconds. ★The sleep calculation does not panic (release = abort · amendment 3)★: sleep = up to the earliest of the deadlines ahead (a full deadline is handled by that tick, so past deadlines are excluded from the count — the plan's sleep ∈ (0, `SCHEDULE_MAX_SLEEP`], 4d2b) · for the reset term, the **integer seconds** of the not-yet-arrived reset's `resets_at − wall()` clamped to (0, `SCHEDULE_MAX_SLEEP`] are carried into `Duration` — since `resets_at` is at most year 9999 (`usage/normalize.rs:18`), the pre-clamp value is not added to `Duration`·`Instant` · the `mono` deadline addition is `saturating_add` (if it overflows it sticks to the end value, so a far-future deadline = the cap sleep · ~~only refusal restoration drops that item with `checked_add`~~ — refusal restoration is removed by ADR-0284, 2026-10-08) · when the system clock is before 1970 (`duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)` is `Err`), `wall()` gives a negative (floor of seconds · no panic — `clock.rs` `epoch_secs`) (during that time every reset reads as the future so the latch does not stand, and ~~refusal restoration is bound by `REJECT_MAX`~~ — removed by ADR-0284, 2026-10-08). Sub-1-second terms such as the merge deadline are left as `Duration` — rounding down to integer seconds would produce a 0-second sleep over and over, spinning idly until the deadline. The driver thread subtracts the time that elapsed after the plan read the clock (including carrying) from that sleep and clamps to [10ms, `SCHEDULE_MAX_SLEEP`] (4d2b) — a deadline that passes while carrying is planned again soon · a wait shorter than 10ms (`schedule.rs` private `SCHEDULE_MIN_SLEEP`) is raised to 10ms so that deadline is at most 10ms late — the purpose = even if a 0 leaks in, do not hammer the book lock without rest. Tests call `tick` directly with a fake clock, and for the driver thread they measure ending (service drop — even during a blocked fake query · sender disconnected · woken with no service) and no strong reference while asleep · re-planning soon on a wake (4d2b).
- **Query driving and shutdown (조회 구동·종료):** a query runs on a detached `std::thread` (name `usage-probe` — the place that launches it is seam `ProbeThreads` · real `OsProbeThreads` · launch `Err` for tests, 4d2a·#89) — it is not the tokio blocking pool, so the runtime drop does not wait, and the child is inside a KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE Job (`platform/windows.rs:4`·`:35`) so when the daemon process ends (normal or crash) the OS reaps the whole tree. So no step is added to graceful shutdown (`lib.rs:754-782`) (§3 #39). ★The query thread holds the service only as `Weak<UsageService>` (amendment 3)★ — the completion guard upgrades once at the end to apply, emit and wake, and if the upgrade fails (the service is already gone) the result is discarded. It also does not hold its own copy of the wake sender — if it held `Arc` or the sender, the scheduler could not see `Disconnected` while a blocked query runs and 「서비스 drop → 스케줄러 스레드 끝」 would be pushed back to the query deadline. The bus wait is the std channel `recv_timeout`, so it has nothing to do with runtime timers (「버스」 below). There are three who start a query — the scheduler · ⟳ (`refresh`) · the bus — and the start, join and completion guard are one set.
- **Pickup path (straight from the pump thread — user decision 2026-09-27, replaces the old waiting map):** the new decorator `UsageObserveSink` (`observe.rs`) receives `usage_observed` and calls `UsageService::observe(obs)` **on the spot (the pump thread)** — there is no map that accumulates. The service does the 「줍기 관측」 row above inside the book lock, and after releasing the lock emits only when something changed. Emit is non-blocking — the real exit does only `FrameSink::try_send` (non-blocking only `net/src/frame_port.rs:43-50`). So the old waiting-map devices (per-cell merging · the mono guard N5 of merged items · the 「합친 결과 = 차례 적용 결과」 property test) are all gone — sequential application is the structure. All other hooks are passed inward. `DaemonStatusSink` is not touched — the 「인코딩해 흘리기만」 (`status_fanout.rs:12-15`) at its head stays true, and `a_usage_observation_is_not_broadcast` (`status_fanout.rs:292-310`) also stays true: ★what emits the usage event is not `DaemonStatusSink` but the exit of the subscribed connection (`ConnUsageOutlet`, 「발행」 below)★ — it emits the merged snapshot, not the raw observation, only to subscribed connections. Only the rationale in that test doc (「요청형이라」) is fixed in Stage 5 to 「원본은 여기서 안 흘린다 — 병합된 스냅숏은 서비스가 구독한 연결에 낸다」. Chain = `MessagingFlushSink(UsageObserveSink(DaemonStatusSink))` (`lib.rs:275-279`) — both decorators pass the new hook (including the parallel branch's `inputs_drained`) (decorator contract `messaging_host.rs:883-888`).
- **Emit (발행) (amendment 1 user decision → in amendment 2 the target and trigger changed — §3 #47·#48·#59·#60):** port `UsageOutlet{ send(&self, frame: &UsageFrame) }` (amendment 11 — #87) — it is **one connection's exit**. `UsageFrame` = the `{snapshot, json: Arc<str>}` built once per `subscribed` in one emit — the service builds JSON through the encoder port `UsageEncoder{ encode(&self, &snapshot, subscribed: &BTreeSet<UsageVendorKey>) -> Option<Arc<str>> }` **after releasing the registry lock** (★amendment 4 — main decision: the 「명부 락 안에서 한 번」 of amendment 3 was moved outside the lock★ · targets are still collected inside the registry lock, and if the collected targets are empty it is not called · ★amendment 6: the event carries the set of the connection that receives it, so once = once per distinct set the targets carry★ — in production with one shell attached it is still once per emit, and even with several targets of the same set it is once · `None` = encoding failure → that share is skipped — the real one logs the failure itself like `event_json` `connection_core.rs:819-827` · being outside the lock, the build and send order of two concurrent emits can differ from the application order — that inversion is already told apart by the shell's highest-revision cache and the frontend revision (§1-7·§1-8) · since the wire variant is only established in Stage 5, the Stage 4 service receives it through a port). The consumer (the service) owns the two traits, the exit implementation is given by the connection layer at `on_connect`, and the encoder implementation by assembly (the old all-connection port `UsagePublisher` and implementation `UsageFanout` are replaced). Stage 4 tests use recording fakes (the encoder fake counts calls). Real (Stage 5): the encoder = moves the **same snapshot** as the bus row into the wire `UsageLimitSnapshot`, puts the set into `subscribed` with the same conversion as `vendor` (canonical key → `AgentBackendKind` — §1-6) → `AgentEvent::UsageLimitsUpdated{snapshot, subscribed}` → `event_json` (`connection_core.rs:820` — `None` is the encoder's `None` as is) · exit `ConnUsageOutlet(Arc<dyn FrameSink>)` (`agent_conn.rs`) = `try_send(Frame::Text(json 사본))` (→ amendment 11: cell `Mutex<Option<Arc<dyn FrameSink>>>` + `revoke` — #87) — no new transport, it is the same per-connection exit as the agent output subscription sink. ★Targets = only connections that subscribed to that company★ — the all-connection fan-out (`fanout.broadcast_text`) is not used: the meaning of subscription would vanish, and a different `subscribed` per connection cannot be carried in one sheet (amendment 6). The cost = the per-connection `try_send` **ends that connection** when saturated (asymmetric `net/src/frame_port.rs:43-50`) — this is the same treatment as the agent output sink, and differs from the list broadcast (`net/src/ws.rs:210-221`) that skips only that connection when saturated (§6 #24).
  - **Trigger = whenever the revision rises:** a pickup application changed something · a query started (`in_flight = true`) · a query ended · a latch set (scheduler · request · pickup · subscription add) · a subscription added (one sheet to that connection only — the table above). With no subscribers there is simply nowhere to send, and the book is still updated.
  - **Only pickup is merged (`USAGE_PUBLISH_COALESCE = 1초`, per company):** ★amendment 12 — it is not a source rule: it is a rate control that suppresses a burst of value changes entering through the observation entrance (`observe` — the stream) (§3 #88)★. The cell holds `last_publish_mono` and `publish_pending`. When pickup changed something, if 1 second has passed since the previous emit, emit at once · otherwise only stand `publish_pending = true` and wake the scheduler → the scheduler emits one snapshot of that moment at `last_publish + 1초` (★final emit guaranteed★ — the last change is not buried). When any emit goes out, erase `publish_pending` and stamp `last_publish` anew (amendment 10 — #86). Query start, end and latch are not merged — they are rare and 「갱신 중」 must not be late.
  - Only the number of those who use the exit grows; the core→daemon branch is still just one `StatusSink` (ADR-0028).
- **Clock (R29·R32):** seam `UsageClock{ mono() → 잠든 시간 포함 단조 경과, wall() → epoch 초 }`. Cooldown · refusal wait · value age = `mono` (the ⟳ interval was removed in amendment 15) · reset comparison · ~~refusal file~~ = `wall` (the refusal file was removed by ADR-0284 — 2026-10-08). The real `mono` is one function, and if there is an OS difference it splits only inside it (§6 #2). If, after resume, the scheduler wakes (at the latest `SCHEDULE_MAX_SLEEP`) and sees a passed deadline, it does one query — not one per missed interval · requests in between join.
- **Expiry latch (R32):** `UsageBook` owns it per window — evaluated when the scheduler (wakes at the `resets_at` of a subscribed company) · request · pickup application · subscription add runs, and once set it holds even if the wall clock is rewound, and only that window's new value (~~pickup `used_pct` · active success~~ new `used_pct` — regardless of source, amendment 12 §3 #88) releases it. It emits when set. The frontend only draws.
- **Refusal storage (거절 저장) (R29) — removed by ADR-0284 (2026-10-08 · user decision · §3 #109):** the refusal deadline is kept only in daemon memory, and after a restart it starts with no deadline. The old `usage_rejects.json` is neither read nor deleted. What follows is the design before removal — ~~`reject_store.rs` — `trait RejectStore{load, save}` · real `FileRejectStore` = `usage_rejects.json` `{schema_version:1, entries:[{vendor, account_key, reject_until_epoch_s}]}` of the daemon data folder (`lib.rs:61-63`) · tmp+rename (`persistence/presets.rs:18-20` precedent). `vendor` is written only with canonical keys, and on reading it is normalized with `usage_probe_for(..)?.key()` (hand-edited case lands in the same cell · unknown word → dropped). Restoration = only `until > wall_now`, remaining time = `min(until − wall_now, REJECT_MAX)`, `checked_add` on `mono` (if it overflows, that item is dropped + warn — release is `panic = "abort"` so overflow during assembly would be an abort on every boot, root `Cargo.toml:35`). ★A restored cell also carries a detail (amendment 3)★ — state `Rejected` + `{kind: "rate_limited", code: None, upstream: None}`: the file does not carry a classification or raw text, so it is filled with the `kind_word()` of `ProbeError::RateLimited` (so 「비정상 상태는 모두 detail 을 든다」 stands even after a restart). Past items ignored · broken file = empty table + warn · write failure = continue after warn. ★Storage = re-snapshot the book inside the dedicated `save_lock` and write everything★ — lock order `save_lock → book` (the book is still a leaf: do not take `save_lock` while holding the book) — even if Claude and Codex refusals occur at the same time, one is not erased by the other. **Tension (accepted):** only a wait that crosses a restart relies on the wall clock.~~
- **Assembly (조립):** only `build_daemon_wiring` (`lib.rs:246-261`) hands over the real implementations — ~~`FileRejectStore` (next to `FilePresetStore::new` `:253`)~~ (removed by ADR-0284 — 2026-10-08) + the real querier (`backend::usage_probes()` + `OsProbeSpawner`) + `threads: Arc::new(OsProbeThreads)` + `scratch_root` (data folder `usage-probe/` — §1-3) (4d2a·#89 — passed to `UsageService::new` with `UsageParts` · §1-4 head). `build_daemon_wiring_with_store` (`:264`) takes **the store and the querier as arguments** — the test server (`:861`) passes an in-memory store and a fake querier, so a test has no way to launch a real CLI. ~~With the state that function `load`s synchronously (4d2a·#89: the store of `UsageParts` is storage-only — reading and `restore_rejects` are still done by assembly) and~~ (refusal reading and restoration removed by ADR-0284 — 2026-10-08) with the real encoder (amendment 3 — the test assembly also uses the real one) it builds `UsageService`, and after launching the scheduler thread (amendment 2 — even if the service outlives the whole assembly, the thread is `Weak` so it ends on the service drop), `UsageObserveSink` is inserted into the chain, `usage` cell in `DaemonWiring` (`:301`) · `AgentConnections::new` (`agent_conn.rs:455`) → `ConnectionCore`. The exit stands per connection, not in assembly — `on_connect` (`agent_conn.rs:195-214`) next to the command registry `attach` (`:215` — **after** the two greetings, for the same reason `:206-213`) does `usage.attach(conn_id, ConnUsageOutlet(frames.clone()))` (→ amendment 11: cell `Mutex<Option<Arc<dyn FrameSink>>>` + `revoke` — #87) · `on_disconnect` (`:357`) does `usage.detach(conn_id)` right after the command registry `detach`. Assembly (`:609`) comes before `run_accept_loop` (`:738`). The test server's scheduler calls only the fake querier, so it has no way to launch a real CLI.
- **Bus (버스):** the port `UsageCommandHost{get, refresh}` is **synchronous** — registered with `blocking_handler` (`command/src/table.rs:359-364`). Because the daemon entrance (`control/commands.rs:73-91`) drives only handlers that finish at the first poll (`drive_to_completion` `:104-125`, `now_or_never` `:109`), and an async handler that waits becomes `OUTCOME_UNKNOWN` — the entrance is not touched. The daemon adapter is already on the blocking pool (`:64-68`), so it calls the service's synchronous method `request_blocking` — the decision is made in the same book, and if it must wait it puts a std `mpsc` sender on that query's joiner list and waits with `recv_timeout(REPLY_WAIT_MAX)` (the completion guard sends). ★`Handle::block_on(timeout(..))` is not used★ — if the runtime takes down the time driver during shutdown, a tokio timer poll can panic, and in release that is an abort. A std channel does not know the runtime. The agent crate also remains ignorant of tokio. The port grows on `make_daemon_table` (`control/commands.rs:44-49`) → agent `make_table` (`agent/commands.rs:441`) — call sites: production `lib.rs:621`·`:875` · three experiment bins (`priming_smoke.rs:150`·`roundtrip_smoke.rs:458`·`saturation_pilot.rs:760`) · eight tests (`control/commands.rs:290·306·322·471` · `connection_core.rs:2324·3458` · `tests/control_agent.rs:370` · `tests/mail_gate.rs:207`) — outside production, a no-op port. ★Keep the raw text from leaking into the after-deadline result log (amendment 3)★ — a daemon-own command that ends after the bus deadline (7 seconds) cannot carry an answer and is left only in the log, but the success payload of a Write that holds a number is printed whole to `warn` (`command_delivery.rs:1497-1503` · port contract `:1182-1183` 「`Ok` payload 는 로그에 실린다」). Since `usage.refresh` is a Write, if left as is the row's `upstream` sits in the log file (a read `usage.get` does not hold a number so it prints only `debug` without a payload — `:1504-1510`). → ★that one branch (`:1497-1503`) prints after removing, from a copy of the payload, **every JSON object key whose name is `upstream`, regardless of depth** (amendment 4 · Stage 5)★ — the command name is not looked at (the old amendment 3 = only commands whose name starts with `usage.` — it is breached if another command carries the same cell) · write `usage.*` and 「`upstream` 키는 어느 명령이든 걷힌다」 in the 「오늘 실물(`agent.*`)은 id·이름·상태뿐」 (`:1183`) of the port doc. The after-deadline result of a read `usage.get` stays the existing branch, `debug` without a payload — it is the existing rule that tells apart by whether a number was held (`retained`), not a name comparison.

### 1-5. When Claude pickup has no utilization (R14·D12)

If the CLI emits the old shape, Claude does not get a fresh from pickup → the cooldown is not reset, and while subscribed the scheduler queries every 15 minutes. With the 2.1.280 captured shape (`utilization` + `unifiedWindows` — UI §4-2), R14 stands. If only one top-level window arrives, each window becomes fresh one at a time (D12 receives that).

### 1-6. wire (`protocol`) · bus

- **Types (`domain.rs`):** `UsageLimitSnapshot{vendor: AgentBackendKind(domain.rs:209), account_key: String, five_hour/weekly: Option<UsageWindow>, model_scoped: Vec<UsageScopedWindow>, plan: Option<String>, in_flight: bool, state: UsageVendorState, revision: u64}` (`served` is removed from the wire — amendment 7 · the stage that removes it = Stage 4, amendment 8 · below) · `UsageWindow{used_pct: Option<f64>, resets_at: Option<u64>, age_secs: u64, expired: bool~~, source: UsageSourceKind~~}` (★replaced (amendment 12 — §3 #88)★: the `source` cell and `UsageSourceKind` are removed from the protocol — `domain.rs` · re-export `lib.rs` · `bindings/UsageSourceKind.ts` deleted · the user is not shown the source of a value · the `PROTOCOL_VERSION` bump is as is in Stage 5) · `UsageScopedWindow{label, window}` · `UsageVendorState` (`#[serde(tag = "kind")]` — `domain.rs:167` precedent) = `Ready | NotInstalled{detail} | NeedsLogin{detail} | Unavailable{detail} | Failed{next_attempt_in_secs: u64, detail} | Rejected{retry_in_secs: u64, detail}` (six — `Unavailable` is 4-0 · the `u64` switch of `next_attempt_in_secs` is a 4-0 decision · the code is Stage 4 piece 4a — currently `u64` · `detail` is 4-0b) · ~~`UsageSourceKind{Passive, Active}`~~ (removed — amendment 12) (`UsageServed{Fresh, Cached}` is moved from here to the daemon `usage_service` — amendment 8, below). u64 gets `#[ts(type = "number")]` (`domain.rs:424`).
  - **`detail: Option<UsageStateDetail>` (4-0b — all five abnormal states):** `UsageStateDetail{kind: String, code: Option<i64>, upstream: Option<String>}` — `kind` = classification word (§1-1 `UsageDetail` as is · e.g. `rate_limits_null`·`claude_error`·`rpc_error`·`limits_unavailable`·`timeout`) · `code` = upstream number (Codex JSON-RPC `error.code` — `#[ts(type = "number | null")]`, ts-rs default i64 = bigint) · `upstream` = upstream raw text (covering · control-character replacement · 200 characters — §1-4 「실패 추적」). ★`kind` is a string, not an enum★ — so that even when the classifications grow, an old shell never fails to read a whole snapshot (the frontend does not translate it and shows it as is). The tag `kind` (state) and the cell `detail.kind` (classification) are on different layers and do not collide. ★`Debug` is implemented by hand and covers `upstream` with `<N자>`★ — `Serialize`·`PartialEq` are as is (wire and tests see the raw text), so wherever the shell or daemon prints it with `{:?}`, the raw text does not appear in the log.
  - **`revision` (the cell newly entering in Stage 5):** the monotonic number of that cell (`UsageKey`) (§1-4) — carried by broadcasts and the shell cache pull (the ⟳ answer is `Ack` — amendment 6). A daemon restart = from 0 again (memory only). The frontend keeps only the highest per company (§1-8). ★No daemon identifier is carried★ — if one client attaches to several daemons, one connection and one subscription is one daemon, and the receiving side's key = (connection, company, `account_key`) (two daemons of the same account each query — §6 #12).
  - **`served` — a broadcast is always `Cached`:** `served` is the cell that speaks of 「이 요청이 기다린 조회가 받아 온 값인가」 (`UsageServed` doc — currently the daemon `usage_service/mod.rs`), so a broadcast with no request has no basis for `Fresh` — the freshness of a value is carried by each window's `age_secs`~~·`source`~~ (amendment 12 — §3 #88). The frontend does not draw `served` (the place that reads it = the bus row). ★Amendment 7: the `served` cell is removed from the wire snapshot (§3 #81)★ — since in amendment 6 the ⟳ answer became `Ack`, the wire snapshot flows only through broadcasts and the shell cache, so that cell is always `Cached` and the frontend does not read it. `served` remains only inside the daemon — the request decision returns `UsageServed` together alongside the snapshot (`served = …` of the §1-4 decision table) and the bus row `UsageVendorRow.served` carries it. ★The type `UsageServed` is also moved from the protocol to the daemon `usage_service` (amendment 8)★ — the bus row lives in the agent crate and agent does not depend on protocol (`crates/engram-dashboard-agent/Cargo.toml:17-18` 「protocol 을 여기 추가하지 말 것」), and the row's `served` is its own macro enum `Fresh|Cached` (「버스」 below). Then the only reader of protocol's `UsageServed` (`protocol/src/domain.rs:513`) is the daemon decision, so a daemon-internal type would sit in the wire crate carrying an unused TS export (`bindings/UsageServed.ts`). ★Stage 4 removes the cell and the type together★ — the cell of `UsageLimitSnapshot` (`domain.rs:444` — cell `:455`) · `UsageServed` (`:513`) · fixture (`:640`) · golden key (`:670`) · ts declaration list (`:732`) · re-export (`protocol/src/lib.rs:40`) · delete `bindings/UsageServed.ts` + regenerate `UsageLimitSnapshot.ts`. Since nobody reads the wire before Stage 5, removing it in Stage 4 keeps the build and behavior standing. Stage 5 fixes the `in_flight` (`domain.rs:453-454` — 「결과는 다음 요청의 답에」) doc and the module head 「요청형」 (`domain.rs:433`) to fit the subscription type.
- **Key conversion and normalization:** the webview word is lower case (`AgentBackendKind` `rename_all = "lowercase"` `domain.rs:207-208`), and the bus word starts with a capital (`AgentBackend` — `commands.rs:71`). Both entrances find the probe with `usage_probe_for(낱말)` (case-insensitive), and the book and the refusal file (removed by ADR-0284 — 2026-10-08) use **that probe's `key()` alone** as the key — the two entrances hit the same cell. The answer's `vendor` is carried by deserializing that key with `AgentBackendKind`. No new vendor match is placed in the daemon.
- **Time representation:** the only absolute time is the single `resets_at` the server gave. Age, wait and next attempt are **relative seconds as of the moment the snapshot was taken (answer · broadcast)** (daemon `mono`) — the frontend adds the time elapsed since the `performance.now()` taken at the moment of receipt (R32).

| Direction | variant (`messages.rs`) | Pair |
|---|---|---|
| client→daemon | `UsageSubscribe{vendors: Vec<AgentBackendKind>}` (amendment 2) — **replaces the whole** subscription set of that connection · empty array = unsubscribe · disconnect = empty set | no answer — no `request_id` (the `Resize`·`Subscribe` form) · for each newly added company one `UsageLimitsUpdated` **to that connection only** (carrying the post-replacement set as `subscribed` — amendment 6) |
| client→daemon | `RefreshUsageLimits{vendor: AgentBackendKind, request_id}` (⟳) | answer = the existing `Ack{request_id}` (`messages.rs:371-373` — at the point where decision and waiting end) + the value is broadcast to subscribers. ★Amendment 6: the dedicated answer `UsageLimits` that carried a snapshot is removed★ — since amendment 5 the webview no longer read that snapshot (it only resolves `pending` — §1-8), the shell passes it through the generic path as is, and the bus does not use this wire — nobody reads it |
| daemon→subscribed connection | `UsageLimitsUpdated{snapshot: UsageLimitSnapshot, subscribed: Vec<AgentBackendKind>}` (amendment 1 broadcast → amendment 2 target = connections that subscribed to that company → ★amendment 6: `subscribed` = the current company set of the receiving connection — §1-4 「구독 명부」 · shell reconciliation §1-7★) | none — an event with no `request_id` (the `PresetListUpdated` `messages.rs:476-478` form) |

- ★There is no wire `GetUsageLimits` (amendment 2 — §3 #67)★ — the webview receives values by subscription and only requests ⟳ · the LLM uses the bus `usage.get` · the shell hands off from the cache (the choice of §3 #62 — if reversed it will be revived, amendment 5). Nobody would call it.
- ★No subscribe/unsubscribe paired messages are set (user accepted 2026-09-27 · §3 #54)★ — if one unsubscribe sheet is lost, the query keeps running. With a whole-set replacement, the last message is the state.
- **`UsageSubscribe` classification:** `command_request_id` (`messages.rs:769`) is **the `None` branch** (`:809-815` next to `Resize`·`Subscribe`·`Unsubscribe`) — since it is a command with no answer, the shell does not put up a waiting ticket — ★it does not send the value through `send_fire_and_forget` (`src-tauri/src/daemon_client/mod.rs:822-824`); the connection task reads the interest when it sends and writes it straight to the socket (amendment 3 — §1-7 「한 직렬 경로」)★ (`forward_daemon_command` would go down the fire-and-forget path if `None` (`commands/agent.rs:234-237`), but a `UsageSubscribe` sent by the webview is blocked before that — below). ★`dispatch_order` (`connection_core.rs:228-275`) is `InOrder`★ — the only basis for detaching is 「순서가 뜻을 갖지 않는다」 (`:202-224`), but for this command order is the meaning (two replacements that arrive back to back must stand in arrival order so that the last one wins). The handling is only latch evaluation + registry replacement + (after releasing the lock) first snapshot and latch emit `try_send` + wake `try_send`, so there is no await and it does not hold the line (§1-4 lock order per path). `inbound_lane` (`:191-199`) is `_ => Ordered` as is.
- **`RefreshUsageLimits`:** add a branch to `command_request_id` — the answer is the existing `Ack`, so `event_reply_request_id` (`:827` — `Ack` branch `:829`) is not touched (amendment 6 — the old paired answer `UsageLimits` is gone). ★`dispatch_order` is `Detached`★ — it waits at most `REPLY_WAIT_MAX`, so if it were left on the line, `WriteStdin` etc. of the same connection would be pushed back. `UsageLimitsUpdated` is **the `None` branch** of `event_reply_request_id` (`:860-861` next to `PresetListUpdated`) — that match has no wildcard, so omitting it stops the compile. `dispatch_order` classifies only **incoming commands** (`AgentCommand`), so it does not apply to events.
- The shell carries the ⟳ request through the generic path, and the connection task writes the subscription first to the same socket just before writing that frame (amendment 7 — replaces the separately inserted forced nudge of the old amendment 6 · §1-7 「⟳」) — `forward_daemon_command` (`src-tauri/src/commands/agent.rs:207`) has no variant allowlist (`messages.rs:559-563`). The reply cap is 30 seconds (`agent.rs:263`). ★`UsageSubscribe` is blocked by the shell, the opposite way★ — the authority over the subscription set is the interest the shell computes from the layout (§1-7), so a set the webview sent must not overwrite it: one branch is added to the same `matches!` (`agent.rs:222-232` — daemon subscription ownership = shell, BLOCK-1) that blocks `Subscribe`/`Unsubscribe`. ★Broadcast takes new code★ — the broadcast of the Text branch is handed by `emit_broadcast` (`src-tauri/src/daemon_client/connection.rs:1228-1266`) to the notification port in a per-variant arm, and an unknown variant is **silently dropped** by `_ => {}` (`:1264`) → §1-7.
- **`PROTOCOL_VERSION` = the master value at merge time + 1 — it is needed.** Because of the new request variant: with an old daemon + new shell, subscription and ⟳ would fail every time and the slot would silently stay empty — the standard of `protocol/src/lib.rs:70-103` (silent malfunction). The broadcast variant · `revision`·`detail`·`subscribed` (amendment 6) cell are also carried at the same number. master is already 6 (parallel-branch merge `0a67566` — §5).
- **Bus (버스) (agent `commands.rs` block, catalog = master value at merge time + 1):** `usage.get {backend: AgentBackend}` → `UsageVendorRow` (Read) · `usage.refresh {backend: AgentBackend}` → `UsageVendorRow` (Write). For the backend vocabulary, use the block-local enum of `agent.new` as is (`commands.rs:71` · words = `backend_word` `:383-386`). Row = `UsageVendorRow{backend, account_key, plan, windows: Vec<UsageWindowRow>, in_flight, served: Fresh|Cached, state: UsageStateWord, next_attempt_in_secs, retry_in_secs, detail_kind: Option<String>, detail_code: Option<i64>, upstream: Option<String>}` (the last three = the detail of 4-0b spread out — the LLM also traces by classification and raw text · whether the macro accepts a `i64` cell is unconfirmed — §6 #27) · `UsageWindowRow{window: FiveHour|Weekly|ModelWeekly, label, used_pct, left_pct: Option<u64>, resets_at, age_secs, expired}` — ★`left_pct` = the same `floor(clamp(100 − used))` as the UI★. The macro enum is only a string enumeration (`command/src/macros.rs:45`), so the state is spread out as a word + optional cells (word = 1:1 with the six wire states — including `Unavailable`). The bus has neither broadcast nor subscription — it is unchanged regardless of amendments 1 and 2. The `usage.get` of a company nobody subscribes to is also decided at request time (if the deadline has passed, query — it does not go through the scheduler).

### 1-7. shell (`src-tauri`)

- **Slot (슬롯):** `SlotContent::Usage{show_claude: bool, show_codex: bool}` (`layout/types.rs:36-46`, serde default true) · `agent_id()` (`:53-57`) · `resolve_spawn_slot` (`manager.rs:794-801`) as occupancy.
- **Bus:** `SlotContentKind::Usage` (`commands.rs:106-111`) + `show_claude`/`show_codex: Option<bool>` on `layout.setSlotContent` (`:302-309`). `slot_content()` (`:921-940`) is bounced if it is carried although it is not Usage. Usage goes to a new `apply::set_usage_slot` (next to `apply.rs:449`) and **inside the lock** reads `mgr.slot_content` (`manager.rs:586`) and merges — a missing cell = the value if it is already Usage, otherwise true. Like the sibling `set_slot_content`, it receives `SubscriptionSync` and re-syncs inside the same lock — that re-sync also recomputes the usage interest (「관심」 below · amendment 6). shell catalog = value at merge time + 1 (now 8 — `:74`).
- **Link permission (링크 권한) (amendment 14 — Stage 8 implementation correction · §3 #92):** the opener plugin is initialized (`lib.rs:39`) but has no permission → ★a `opener:allow-open-url` narrowed to two URLs in a dedicated file `capabilities/usage-links.json` (windows = `main`·`slot-popup-*`)★ (the implementation did not put it directly in `default.json`·`popup.json` — keeping the two-URL list in one file only prevents drift).
